@@31682336 <h> Here are 25 of the most innovative new projects using tech to help refugees and NGOs <p> From humble beginnings as a simple Facebook group I posted in September 2015 , Techfugees has come a long way . It was conceived as a vehicle to enthuse technologists about the plight of refugees by waking them up to the idea that their innovation , startup mentality and design-led thinking could potentially bring new , scalable new solutions to the plight of displaced people . Today , Techfugees is an international non-profit with its own CEO , Jos ? phine Goube and a team based between London and Paris . Not bad for a handful of posts on social media ... <p> What 's fascinating about the project as it 's developed is that , at the time , it was considered quite radical , perhaps even odd , to bring tech people into the equation . But simply watching the footage of refugees clutching smartphones as they fled war-torn regions and natural disasters made the tech world realize it can be part of the solution to many of the seemingly intractable problems refugees face . <p> Techfugees has grown @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ world , supporting by way of their own projects or companies , via social media and taking part in hundreds of dedicated events around the world . This includes more than 30 hackathons and an annual Global Summit , the second of which happened over the last two days in Paris . The Summit had over 500 participants , such as social entrepreneurs , engineers , designers , humanitarians , policymakers , researchers or impact investors , a large number of whom who have a refugee background . Speakers discussed and debate the different uses of technology for displaced people during the time of migration until arrival to their new host societies . <p> The impact of climate change will cause the migration of 143 million people by 2050 <p> This year 's program looked at four main topics : Access to Rights and Information ; Data Ethics ; Social Inclusion ; and Climate Migration . The last issue is now of even greater urgency in 2018 . According to a study by the World Bank published earlier this year , the impact of climate change will cause the migration @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ looming humanitarian challenges . <p> Just like at your typical tech startup conference , Techfugees has a similar programme : The Techfugees Global Challenges Competition . This showcases projects responding to the needs of displaced populations and building technological products or services for them , based on Techfugees ' 8 guiding principles and addressing one of Techfugees ' five focus area : access to rights and information , health , education , employment and social inclusion . The applications went through an international Jury of experts who selected the 25 finalists from hundreds of applications , from 52 countries across the world , which pitched their project in front of an international Jury and Summit attendees . <p> " Integreat is an information app and website tailored to the specific needs of both newcomers as the users of the app and municipal administrations as the content providers . It 's a mobile guide for newcomers . Multilingual . Offline . Free . Can we provide the people arriving in our city with all relevant information in their native language as quickly as possible ? Even without internet access and without @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Integreat which passes on all relevant information in multiple languages to the newcomers . It is a holistic service ecosystem for cities , districts and organizations for the integration of people with a flight or migration background . " <p> Shifra Australia / USA " Shifra is not only a life-saving mHealth intervention , it is also a research project which aims to explore the social , cultural and geographic barriers to quality healthcare access many refugees experience , as cited by the refugees themselves . The Shifra web app is designed to improve access to quality sexual and reproductive health care . It provides local , evidence-based health information in multiple languages for communities with varying levels of language and health literacy . Shifra also directs users to trusted clinics where they can access respectful and safe care . We work with local health networks to improve their existing services based on the self-identified health needs found in Shifra 's anonymous user trend data . " <p> Antura and the Letters ( Syria , Lebanon , Jordan , Turkey , Iraq and Egypt ) " Antura and the Letters @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ how to read in Arabic and improve their psychosocial well-being . Considering that most refugees have old smartphones and connectivity is always a challenge for them , the game runs on old devices ( from 2010/2011 ) , it 's very small to download ( less than 80Mb on Android ) and it does not require internet connection . Antura and the Letters is completely free and open source ... and it has been designed in order to be easily adaptable to other languages ! That 's exactly what we want to do next with the goal to reach and help as many children as possible around the world . " <p> TaQadam ( Lebanon ) " In the era of machine learning and artificial intelligence , the data workers and annotators are the new programmers . From robots , drones , self-driving cars or e-commerce , the markets need for vision technology for artificial intelligence is extraordinary . One of the major building blocks of such AI-powered recognition systems is image annotation delivered with a human input -- data training . Today 's data is driving tomorrow 's AI @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ on having data-edge often more than a technology-edge , but 80% of data engineers ' time is spent on sourcing and preparing quality image data for AI models . TaQadam optimizes image annotation for data-driven companies with visual AI and delivers on-demand , vertical-specific , high-quality image annotation . With an API and a cloud architecture , we ensure a simple and secure way to build image data set with a high accuracy and precision , while simplifying the process of sourcing human insights from dedicated and trained teams of TaQadam . TaQadam is a unique service on the market that brings a specialized on use case teams that are building AI together with the client . With gamification and mobile accessible work on TaQadam Android App , we transform the experience of annotation to fit the younger generations . We create work of the future : accessible , flexible , allowing fluidity , community building and fun . " <p> Refugees Are ( Worldwide ) " Refugees Are is a platform to analyze news published globally around refugees , display their daily struggles as mentioned in the news and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ public in classifying their news as " negative " or " positive " to raise more awareness in the language used by journalists and politicians when describing refugees and migrants . The goal is to tackle the problem of Xenophobia against refugees caused by the media which is affecting negatively their hope in integrating and starting a new beginning . " <p> And finally The Mohajer App won a special jury prize for its outstanding work assisting Afghan refugees in Iran in incredibly difficult circumstances : <p> The Mohajer AppAndroid / IOS Iran , US , Canada and UK " The Mohajer App was created with the support of Afghan communities inside Iran to address their needs . The app was completed with a group of paid and voluntary refugee-rights attorneys , advocates and technologists . Mohajer has two features : -- The " Get Informed " section provides information for users concerning Iran 's immigration policy , the rights of Afghans in Iran , and resources that are available for concerns such as health , education , combatting from discrimination and more ; the list continues to expand as @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ list of support groups that our team has verified directly . The " Submit Report " feature enables users to share their everyday experiences as Afghans in Iran and support the larger community in addressing challenges by sharing information on events and experiences . The information on the app is also accessible offline , so as to support those without regular internet access . " <p> Here 's a run down of the rest of the 25 that pitched , in their own words : <p> Challenge #1 -- Access to rights &amp; information <p> TikkTalk ( Norway ) " Tikk Talk is an open marketplace for interpretation services for everyone who is in need for interpretation assistance . So far the platform handles 80% of all assignments automatically , limiting the overhead costs which traditional agencies have . The platform also gives all parties full transparency which empowers them to make better decisions . Because of the tech , interpreters are in the forefront deciding on their wage and which assignments they would like to take . Before , Helse F ? rde ( Hospital partner ) switched to @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ 99% qualified interpreters . " <p> " Refugee Info Bus 's mission is simple . Operating at the frontlines of Europe 's ongoing refugee crisis , we provide good quality multilingual legal information and free Wifi to refugees on the move in , or having just arrived , in Northern France and in Greece . Our first Refugee Info Bus began life as an old horsebox , purchased , stripped-out , cleaned-up , and converted into a mobile office and Wi-Fi hotspot for refugees and asylum seekers living in northern France . Within a year , we facilitated over 91,000 Wi-Fi logins and delivered more than 1,000 workshops to 50,000+ individuals on the UK and French asylum systems . " <p> " In mid-2016 , Refugee.Info pivoted to focus on social media to better serve the needs and preferences of users , which had drastically changed after borders closed in Europe in March of that year . Refugee.info hired local journalists to obtain and verify news and other up-to-date information about the context , as well as content professionals to optimize the information for social media , applying private sector @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ in Greece , Italy and the Balkans can message the page and receive a quick answer from a moderator who will work with the journalists and lawyers to provide accurate information , often sourced from their website or blog . " <p> Challenge #2 -- Health <p> Connect 2 Drs Mexico The platform of Connect2Drs was initially built to strive the private sector as a target market , and it still is . However , with the injustice and lack of a good health insurance for mexicans -- deported or refugees -- people with disabilities and people who need medical attention at home with palliatives became their main goal . <p> Doctor-X Jordan " Doctor-X is a multi-language medical history mobile application and website with , for each refugee , a private account that the doctor can update when he does an operation on the refugee , in the language the doctor speaks . The program will make it available in 5 languages in case the refugee goes to a new country and needs medical help . " <p> Iryo Jordan " Until now , medical workers in camps used @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ of that , medical workforce turnover is high , bringing additional confusion and inconsistency to Excel records . Iryo enables accurate medical history recording . Because data storage is decentralized with a copy on a local server , a second one on the patients mobile phone and a third one in the Iryo cloud , even if a patient arrives at a new refugee camp where the Iryo system is already in place , the doctor there will be able to access the patient 's record . " <p> MedShr UK/Worldwide " MedShr has been developed to enable doctors and healthcare professionals to share and discuss clinical cases for peer-to-peer learning and medical education . It is a private , professional , verified network for clinical case discussion between medical professionals . No patient information is visible , all cases are anonymous and members can use the mobile app to get consent from patients to share images . Beyond that , all images and media are securely cloud stored with no images stored on the user 's device . Importantly , MedShr members are also able determine who can see @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Education <p> edSeed ( United States , Gaza , Lebanon ) " Edseed is about narrating stories of youth and bringing them closer to donors in the USA ; participating in networking ; and building a network for higher education of refugees to address policy issues , mentor students . " <p> Paper Airplanes ( United States / Turkey , Lebanon , Jordan , KSA , Egypt , Iraq , Palestine ) " Paper Airplanes ( PA ) is a nonprofit that uses video conferencing technology to provide free , peer-to-peer language and professional skills instruction to young adults and teens affected by conflict in the Middle East and North Africa . PA works to support these individuals to pursue their educational and employment goals and ultimately rebuild their lives . PA teaches English and Turkish to youth and adults , journalism to citizen journalists , and beginners ' coding skills to women . By using virtual communication technology to provide live instruction , PA is able to reach internally displaced and refugee youth as well as underserved populations who may be otherwise difficult to reach , including those inside @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ women and girls , and individuals in rural areas across the MENA region . Additionally , PA supplies computer tablets for select Youth Exchange Program participant recipients and scholarships to defray the cost of the IELTS and TOEFL exams for qualified PA graduates . " <p> Power.Coders ( Switzerland ) " Powercoders ' solution is to offer intensive computer programming classes to refugees over a three month period and then place them in an IT internship . As a result of the comprehensive training and subsequent placement , within a little less than a year our refugee graduates are exponentially better positioned to find and keep an IT job in Switzerland , and many do just that . The program is fully customized to address the challenges and issues that refugees may face when trying to integrate professionally and the courses enjoy an almost 100% internship placement success rate and subsequent 80% integration rate . " <p> RefgueeEd.Hub ( Greece ) RefugeeEd.Hub is an open source online database that promotes promising practice in refugee education globally . RefugeeEd.Hub aims to raise the quality of education for refugees and displaced people @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ stakeholders working to provide education to refugees . RefugeeEd.Hub will support education innovators , multilateral institutions , global development actors , education funders and government and policymakers to inform practice on the ground . <p> " Bitae Technologies aims to help global , mobile talent , like refugees and migrants , carry their skills and experience with them in a secure , verified digital CV , addressing the lack of access to formal education and employment faced by refugees and other vulnerable populations . Bitae transforms non-formal learning and achievements into opportunities for refugees . We provide a platform to track , store and verify refugees ' non-formal learning and skills , creating a " digital backpack " of classes , workshops , internships and skills that together , can help a refugee move forward with education and employment . Bitae leverages mobile and blockchain technology to ensure that governments , international organizations , NGOs , educational institutions and employers are able to document non-formal learning and skills in the most inclusive , secure and transparent way . The Digital Backpack focuses on four key functions : creating badges and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ and skills assessment . Using existing tools , the platform makes it possible to create blockchain-backed credential badges that can be stored and shared . " <p> Human in the loop Bulgaria -- 2017 " Human in the Loop is a social enterprise which employs and trains refugees to provide image annotation services to computer vision companies . It is a niche market that currently requires manual human input in order to train ML models to recognize images in a way that a human would , and Human in the Loop is part of a growing community of " impact sourcing " enterprises that is dedicated to providing employment to vulnerable groups in this sector . The opportunity they are seizing is that image annotation is a very accessible type of labor that does not require previous education or professional skills , but which can open the door to more advanced tech jobs and freelancing skills , which are especially useful for migrants . In this way , they are empowering refugees to earn a living in a dignified way and gain skills , and they are turning them in @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ of the opportunities that remote digital work provides to people who are on the move . Human in the Loop works as an outsourcing business with B2B sales . Their clients are companies from the computer vision , self-driving cars , drones , and satellite imager industry , which are training machine learning models . " <p> Rafiqi ( United Kingdom , Germany , Jordan ) " Rafiqi is a matching tool that leverages artificial intelligence to connect refugees in real-time and in a customized way to the opportunities that are the most suitable to his/her profile and that would lead to lifelong employment . Currently , there is no single platform where resettled refugees can access and filter the wide range of opportunities available to them , including jobs , trainings , mentorships and degrees , and where any organization ( company/NGO/university ) can seamlessly access and filter refugee talent . Refugees lack of knowledge of opportunities and of the right opportunities is resulting in them being unemployed or being overqualified for what they are actually doing . Despite the existence of some refugee to jobs matching programs supported @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Netherlands , these matchings remain largely manual and limited in terms of intelligence . These matching efforts can not scale well given the high number of refugees and the diversity of their profiles , as well as the diversity of opportunities available to them . " <p> Transformify Rebuild Lives Program ( Worldwide / EU , Iraq ) " The Rebuild Lives Program by Transformify exists to provide access to jobs and secure payment to displaced people as well as access to targeted eLearning to improve their skills by using recruitment CRM leveraging HR-tech , fintech and AI to connect refugees with employers and provide access to secure payment even if the refugees have no permanent address or a bank account . " <p> Challenge #5 -- Social inclusion <p> PLACE ( France , Germany , United Kingdom ) " PLACE runs Innovation Labs for migrants and refugees in Europe . These labs transform the people from migrants and refugees into Innovators -- creators of solutions for European societies . The labs are 1 to 3-day immersive experiences that apply design thinking methodology to enable Innovators to identify problems , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ and prototype these solutions with a diverse community of local stakeholders . Beyond the Labs , the Innovators have the opportunity to develop their projects through the network of the PLACE collective -- actors in the private , public and civil society that see the value of diversity in migrant-led innovation and who want to be a part of it . In addition to innovative solutions , the labs also produce a new leadership model for Europe . Innovators who demonstrate motivation and willingness to take on a role as a leader in migrant-led innovation are trained to be PLACE Catalysts . The Catalysts are trained in interculturality , sourcing , public speaking , networking and lab facilitation . They are then given the opportunity to apply these learnings as facilitators in Labs throughout Europe . " <p> Register of Pledges ( Ireland ) " The Register of Pledges project workstream are : Humanitarian Database of Pledges ( Accommodation , Goods and Services ) administered by Red Cross with back-office capabilities for pledge management and workflow and reporting capabilities ; Open-source version of the technology is available on Github , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ interface ; Evolve and open-source our Case Management System , to optimize Service User outcomes . " <p> SchoolX ( UK/Turkey ) " SchoolX envision a shared economy model with volunteer teachers which include university students , educated refugees , retired teachers and other local volunteers , who will teach refugee students . Due to the challenge of limited access to education that these displaced people face , our solution is to recruit teachers within the refugee community and local community , and connect them with refugee students who are eager to learn . The talents of these teachers are then harnessed to deliver rigorous and certified education to the students . Through this , volunteers , including refugee teachers , will also receive an allowance for their efforts as well . The solution , in a form of an online platform , will provide training packages that involve not only fundamental tenets of teaching , but also pedagogical and psycho-social training for the volunteers to prepare them to approach refugee children in the most appropriate and empowering manner , The online platform will also serve as a database which @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ their needs , skills , availability , and geographical proximity in order to arrange flexible , face-to-face lessons . " <p> SPEAK ( Portugal , Spain , Italy , Germany ) SPEAK is a crowdsourced language and culture exchange network , based on an Online2Offline model . All processes are managed online , through a platform developed in-house , while the learning and sharing experience happens offline , allowing participants to establish a close relationship with one another . This model ensures a greater efficiency and minimization of fixed costs , allowing SPEAK to be sustainable at scale while charging only a symbolic fee for its program . SPEAK empowers its participants by expanding their language and cultural skills , all the while becoming part of mutual support networks . Through a language and culture exchange , SPEAK connects migrants , refugees and locals living in the same city . In creating bridges between migrants and locals , members often help each other with job offers or renting their first house in a new city thanks to the power of SPEAK communities . These networks are home to a multicultural @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ validated . In other words , SPEAK 's networks nurture unity in diversity . SPEAK 's volunteer Buddy system empowers anyone with the willingness to share their language and culture , allowing for an " everyone a changemaker " attitude , which encourages an even greater participation in local public life . he sustainability of the initiative relies on the community and willingness to promote SPEAK 's values of an integrated and inclusive society . "

According to the &lt;a href= " http : //ubcp.com " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Union of B.C. Performers and ACTRA , &lt;/a&gt; all 42 of the productions they had listed are now shut down . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I don&amp;rsquo ; t know anybody who saw this coming , &amp;rdquo ; said Vancouver actor/producer/director &lt;a href= " https : **30;38878;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;John Cassini&lt;/a&gt; . &amp;ldquo ; I know when there is a writer&amp;rsquo ; s strike or something , you have a lot of lead up and people know , so , you tend to backload or front-load , whatever the term is . Everyone knows , &amp;lsquo ; **34;38910;TOOLONG ... before the strike hits . There&amp;rsquo ; s a little bit of planning . But , here in this case , people were just sent home from set or told it&amp;rsquo ; s not happening &amp;mdash ; all I think within 24-48 hours . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Things were just going , boom , boom , boom . That&amp;rsquo ; s it , full stop . That&amp;rsquo ; s it . There&amp;rsquo ; s no way any of us could have planned for this . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;38946;TOOLONG ... title= " John Cassini said COVID-19 forced production shutdowns . " data-portal-copyright= " Gerry Kahrmann " **28;38982;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1242161 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Cassini , who you can see now on Netflix in the classic Canadian and Vancouver-shot crime drama &lt;a href= " https : **31;39012;TOOLONG ? r ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **26;39045;TOOLONG , was set to start pre-production on a TV movie March 16 . That was tabled . The threat of spreading COVID-19 also led Cassini to temporarily close the doors on the non-profit Railtown Actors Studio that he co-founded eight years ago . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I thought this was going to be a bit of a sanctuary for everyone , &amp;rdquo ; said Cassini about the studio that has three instructors and teaches 70 students each term . &amp;ldquo ; I thought , &amp;lsquo ; OK , the business is going to shut down , maybe , and this is a place where we can come and really dig in to the work . And wait for the business to come back , &amp;rsquo ; but of course , thinking a little deeper it is now that the gatherings have to be smaller , and the gatherings are no more than 12 , 13 , 14 in a class sometimes . But still , I personally felt if I could do anything that&amp;rsquo ; s going to help this is it , besides staying home . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Van Helsing showrunner Jonathan Lloyd Walker of Vancouver returned from shooting the first three episodes of Season 5 of the hit Syfy channel show ready to begin filming the show&amp;rsquo ; s last 10 episodes in Vancouver . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It came on really quickly . Literally , this time last week we were coming back from Slovakia , &amp;rdquo ; said Walker . &amp;ldquo ; While we were shooting in Slovakia there was an awareness that it was happening in places in the world and it was happening on a fairly small scale , in terms of local impact . So , it didn&amp;rsquo ; t really affect us. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But all that changed in an instant when they returned to Vancouver on March 9. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; By the time we got to mid-week all of a sudden things were changing at such a pace I was like , &amp;lsquo ; I don&amp;rsquo ; t know what we are going to do ? &amp;rsquo ; It did come on very suddenly and took us all by surprise . Certainly we were monitoring the situation , then we reached the point when we just had to shut down . We made that decision , officially , over the weekend . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Walker&amp;rsquo ; s show , like many , will re-evaluate the situation in two-week&amp;rsquo ; s time . Others are waiting a month , and some have just ended their shoots early . While two weeks seems like a short period , it isn&amp;rsquo ; t in an industry that already comes packed with volatility and uncertainty . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There are a lot people worried about their livelihood . For instance , a lot of actors and background performers I know that have said , &amp;lsquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; ve got five or six days of booked work and now I have nothing and I live paycheque-to-paycheque , what am I going to do ? &amp;rsquo ; Walker said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; From that standpoint , they are all nervous . They don&amp;rsquo ; t know how long the industry is going to be suspended . There is no work and there won&amp;rsquo ; t be for the foreseeable future . We&amp;rsquo ; re all going to have to calm some nerves and figure out a plan to take care of each other . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The plan now isn&amp;rsquo ; t clear . Industry unions have sent messages to the federal and provincial governments reminding them not to forget about the arts when it comes time to distribute emergency funding . Union websites have posts suggesting members seek out groups that help artists and apply for government benefits . A lot of actors , in particular , are self-employed so they don&amp;rsquo ; t qualify for unemployment benefits , however the Government of Canada just announced its COVID-19 Economic Response Plan . The plan will provide up to $27 billion in direct support to Canadian workers and businesses including those self-employed within the film industry . &amp;nbsp ; The unions have also told members that upcoming dues deadlines will be pushed back and reviewed over the course of this economic downturn . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We are dealing with the situation regarding COVID-19 as it unfolds in real time . With things changing so quickly we can&amp;rsquo ; t comment on any specifics . This is an unprecedented situation , not only locally but globally , and we continue to work in the best interest of the professional artists and technicians of the ( International Alliance of Theatrical Stage Employees ) Local 891 through this difficult time , &amp;rdquo ; said IATSE spokesman Phil Klapwyk in an email to Postmedia News . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The community , of course , reaches well beyond the faces you see on the screen . It literally takes a village to make a TV show or film . The tens-of-thousands of people the industry employs around B.C. include food-vendors , drivers , rental companies , special effects people , hair and makeup folks , and on and on . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; In this industry , historically , people are always a bit jumpy about when the next job is coming because you don&amp;rsquo ; t always know . But , in the last few years , we have never had to really worry about that , &amp;rdquo ; said Vancouver TV/film hairstylist Jim Dreichel ( See and The Chilling Adventures of Sabrina ) , referring to the rebound in the industry after a slow period about seven years ago . &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s been massive . Most of us turn down work all the time because we are already working . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Now there is nothing . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Co-owner Glenn Ennis of Vancouver specialty camera team Peacemaker Filmworks ( Deadpool 1 &amp;amp ; 2 , The Predator , Arrow , Supergirl ) said the company wrapped a major car commercial March 15 , and now is looking at two months of nada. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;39073;TOOLONG ... title= " Peacemaker Filmworks co-owner and stunt actor Glenn Ennis is one of the many , many people in the TV/film-production business in B.C. who have seen work disappear due to COVID-19. " alt= " Peacemaker Filmworks co-owner and stunt actor Glenn Ennis is one of the many , many people in the TV/film-production business in B.C. who have seen work disappear due to COVID-19. Some other centre picks up the slack when somewhere else can&amp;rsquo ; t work for whatever reason . But this one is worldwide , and a complete unknown , &amp;rdquo ; said Ennis , who&amp;rsquo ; s also a stunt actor who has now lost a lot of work . &amp;ldquo ; A lot of people live paycheque-to-paycheque in the film world , depending on what part of it you are in and it can be pretty nasty . I mean , at the best of times you don&amp;rsquo ; t know when your next job is. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ennis and company are trying to figure out a way to keep some of their lights on and equipment running . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; At Peacemaker , we are trying to reach out and offer up our services to produce stuff in-house on our own . For instance , we can remotely do a car commercial for somebody , &amp;rdquo ; said Ennis , referring to a system that allows them to send footage over cellphone signals . This way a director can see footage , wherever they are , in real time . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We could potentially go out and shoot a car commercial , which we have done a million times and we are good at it , and in real time people could be sitting in their agency office in New York and watching the filming and then by phone say , &amp;lsquo ; Yeah , that looks great but can you get it from another angle&amp;rsquo ; &amp;hellip ; We have done that before when a director was unavailable . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; So yeah , we&amp;rsquo ; re trying to get people to look at other ways to do things in order to carry on , &amp;rdquo ; added Ennis. &amp;ldquo ; But , at this point , it is pretty early in the process to figure out what is going to evolve from this in the short-term . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Well-known Vancouver stage and screen actor Brian Markinson ( Mad Men , Girlfriends&amp;rsquo ; Guide to Divorce , the L Word , DaVinci&amp;rsquo ; s Inquest ) says he&amp;rsquo ; s lucky that his three-decade-long busy career has allowed him to save and buy a house , but he still feels the pinch when jobs are gone . He lost a gig thanks to this shutdown and remembers what it was like having to live paycheque-to-paycheque. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s tough , &amp;rdquo ; said Markinson , who has had to cancel teaching gigs , as well . &amp;ldquo ; But I know we will be there for each other . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;39139;TOOLONG ... title= " Vancouver actor Brian Markinson hopes that out of the darkness of the COVID-19 crisis great art will be created . " alt= " Vancouver actor Brian Markinson hopes that out of the darkness of the COVID-19 crisis great art will be created . " data-portal-copyright= " Jeff Weddell Photography " **28;39175;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1241356 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Over his 30-year career Markinson has read many , many scripts and doesn&amp;rsquo ; t hesitate when asked if this current crisis would be inspiration for a Hollywood movie . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think this is going to have to be a miniseries . I don&amp;rsquo ; t think a movie will hold it , &amp;rdquo ; said Markinson , adding that tough times have a history of inspiring artists to create . &amp;ldquo ; Out of these crises , that&amp;rsquo ; s when great art comes . Great scripts came out of the WPA ( U.S. New Deal Works Progress Administration ) and the Great Depression and the Vietnam War era . Shakespeare , it&amp;rsquo ; s said , wrote King Lear during the plague . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; So hopefully , maybe great art will come out of this time . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;39205;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **32;39232;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/danagee " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **29;39266;TOOLONG &; ... &lt;/p&gt; Dana GeeThu , 19 Mar 2020 19:41:06 **45;39297;TOOLONG ... **28;39344;TOOLONG : More Vancouver-based TV productions shut downhttp : **36;39374;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;39412;TOOLONG ... title= " A number of Vancouver TV productions are shutting down due to the COVID-19 outbreak . " data-portal-copyright= " Malcolm Parry " **28;40265;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1236514 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; FIGHTING FENTANYL : This column recently expected COVID-19 to inspire another epidemic-oriented novel from city-based Daniel Kalla . Meanwhile , his 10th book , The Last High ( Simon &amp;amp ; Schuster Canada ) , addresses the fentanyl-driven opioid crisis that has killed close to 14,000 Canadians . Picturing the experiences of victims , dealers , police and especially hospital staff , Kalla provides absorbing procedural accuracy from his years heading emergency surgery at St. Paul&amp;rsquo ; s Hospital ( St. Michael&amp;rsquo ; s in the book ) . An amusing personal reference in the novel has toxicologist-ER doctor Julie Rees relate her fictional colleagues&amp;rsquo ; secondary interests : &amp;ldquo ; One guy even writes books &amp;mdash ; medical thrillers &amp;mdash ; they&amp;rsquo ; re not half bad . &amp;rdquo ; Not half bad , indeed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; DOWN PARRYSCOPE : I kiss the ground today , March 14 , the anniversary of my being welcomed to ever-peerless British Columbia . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **27;40295;TOOLONG " **34;40324;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; 604-929-8456 &lt;/p&gt; Malcolm ParryFri , 13 Mar 2020 14:06:10 **45;40360;TOOLONG ... ArtsLocal BusinessLocal HealthMedicine MattersMoviesSmall **34;40407;TOOLONG music : Vancouver Symphony Orchestra pays tribute to Beethovenhttp : **31;40443;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;40476;TOOLONG ... title= " VSO composer-in residence Rodney Sharman 's Archaic Smile will feature in Beethoven The Modernist ( March 7 at the Orpheum ) " alt= " VSO composer-in residence Rodney Sharman 's Archaic Smile will feature in Beethoven The Modernist ( March 7 at the Orpheum ) " data-portal-copyright= " Andrew Shopland " **28;40512;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1222242 " /&gt; &lt;h3&gt;The Beethoven Experience&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;When : &lt;/strong&gt; March 7&amp;ndash ; 21 &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Where : &lt;/strong&gt; Various locations &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Info and tickets : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **27;40542;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **30;40571;TOOLONG &; ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s Beethoven Year &amp;mdash ; the 250th anniversary of Ludwig van&amp;rsquo ; s birth . There are any number of special events designed to commemorate this , including the Vancouver Symphony&amp;rsquo ; s Beethoven Experience set of concerts and events March 7 through 21. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But , I hear you ask , isn&amp;rsquo ; t every classical music season a Beethoven year ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;40603;TOOLONG ... title= " Ludwig van Beethoven . " alt= " Ludwig van Beethoven . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;40639;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1223982 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 1959 , the great Leonard Bernstein talked about our fixation with the composer in his book The Joy of Music . In one of his landmark essays he noted , with more than a hint of exasperation : &amp;ldquo ; Ever since I can recall , the first association that springs to anyone&amp;rsquo ; s mind when serious music is mentioned is &amp;lsquo ; Beethoven&amp;rsquo ; . When you walk into a concert hall bearing the names of the greats inscribed around it on a frieze , there he sits , front and centre , the first , the largest , the most immediately visible , and usually gold-plated. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yet Lenny had his answers about why Beethoven&amp;rsquo ; s stature remains larger than life , and I have a few ideas too : he&amp;rsquo ; s that good , that radical , that human , that uplifting . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Good thoughts to keep in mind during a year of multiple helpings of the great master&amp;rsquo ; s work , mostly good , sometimes bad , rarely indifferent . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What has the VSO got in store for us , and how will it add to our understanding of LvB ? I think it&amp;rsquo ; s all about creating a nuanced understanding of context : what his music meant in his own time , and how his legacy evolved through the ages up to the present day . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The centre of the Beethoven Experience is two concerts , &lt;strong&gt;A Most Remarkable Night , Parts 1 and 2&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;em&gt; ( March 13 and 14 at the Orpheum ) &lt;/em&gt; . This is a two-evening duplication of a single early 19th century program the VSO describes as &amp;ldquo ; an astounding four-hour concert that saw the premi&amp;egrave ; res of both the 5th and 6th Symphonies , the Choral Fantasy , and his Piano Concerto No. 4 , along with an improvised piano fantasy &amp;mdash ; Beethoven conducting and playing all of it . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This program is pragmatically stretched out over two evenings to suit 21st century audiences , for whom a four-hour-plus extravaganza would be too much of a good thing . Placing three masterworks in proximity enables a new way of hearing ultrafamiliar repertoire , just like conductor Otto Tausk&amp;rsquo ; s trilogy of late Mozart symphonies last season . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;In the Heart of the Symphony&lt;/strong&gt; ( &lt;em&gt;March 11 ) &lt;/em&gt; , takes the renowned Fifth Symphony to unfamiliar turf for the orchestra : the heritage Commodore Ballroom . The strategy is bring the audience up close and real personal with the orchestra , and I think it&amp;rsquo ; s a simple , great idea . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tracing how the Beethoven myth grew and mutated over time is another aspect of the Beethoven Experience . **27;40669;TOOLONG : Chamber Concert&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;em&gt; ( March 9 , Christ Church Cathedral ) &lt;/em&gt; &amp;nbsp ; will include a performance of Brahms&amp;rsquo ; s landmark Piano Quintet . It demonstrates how Beethoven impressed the best and brightest of the mid-19th century composers , and also includes baritone Tyler Duncan singing Beethoven&amp;rsquo ; s An die ferne Geliebte. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The decision to reprise former VSO composer-in residence Rodney Sharman&amp;rsquo ; s Archaic Smile in &lt;strong&gt;Beethoven The Modernist&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;em&gt; ( March 7 at the Orpheum ) &lt;/em&gt; , shows that contemporary figures still feel a connection to Beethoven as the grandfather ( I use the gender intentionally ! ) of the radical tradition . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **25;40698;TOOLONG Eroica&lt;/strong&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;em&gt; ( March 20 and 21 at the Chan Centre ) , &lt;/em&gt; led by Australian-born composer , conductor and violist Brett Dean offers another object lesson in how today&amp;rsquo ; s composers still think of Beethoven as almost a contemporary . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dean&amp;rsquo ; s program includes the Eroica Symphony and his own Beethoven-themed Testament . Provocatively , and the concert starts with Die grosse Fuge &amp;mdash ; a contrapuntal behemoth originally designed as the ending of an already elephantine string quartet ; Beethoven&amp;rsquo ; s idiosyncratic homage to the fugue will played here by a full string orchestra . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As with the VSO New Music festivals , the hope is that audience members see themselves as participants rather than passive listeners . To that end , various discounted series pass options are on offer . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;40725;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;40767;TOOLONG ... about the VSO&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;40803;TOOLONG ... about classical **26;40839;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;40867;TOOLONG ... for something to do ? Davis " **28;41489;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1223647 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Clements&amp;rsquo ; film will open the 15th annual Vancouver International Women in Film Festival , which takes place at the Vancity Theatre from March 3 to 8 . The film is one of nine feature films at the festival . Also on the program are seven documentaries and 26 short films . In the festival there are eight Canadian premieres . The Argentinian drama The Rabbit&amp;rsquo ; s House about a young girl living with activists in the mid seventies will have its world premiere at VIWFF. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s always an honour to be included in a festival that celebrates women filmmakers , &amp;rdquo ; said Clements , a Metis multiple disciplined artist who lives on Galiano Island . &amp;ldquo ; Being able to be in a community and to see other women&amp;rsquo ; s work and to see , you know , what they&amp;rsquo ; re making and see what stories they&amp;rsquo ; re really passionate about , I think it just feeds you . There&amp;rsquo ; s a feeling when you&amp;rsquo ; re at these festivals , yes you&amp;rsquo ; re there for your baby , but you really are being inspired and kind of motivated , propelled by what other artists are doing and how they got to do it . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;41519;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;41555;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Put on by the Women in Film and Television Vancouver organization , the VIWFF not only celebrates great cinema made by women but it offers a full slate of supportive programming including panels , seminars , artist talks , pitch sessions , a screenplay competition , social events and an awards gala . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I really think it&amp;rsquo ; s just like an incubator of ideas and possibilities , &amp;rdquo ; added Clements . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The concept for Red Snow , which also gets it theatrical release in &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Ottawa , Vancouver and Toronto on March 13th , &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; goes back a decade to when Clements was looking at photos of Canadian soldiers in Afghanistan . In the pictures were often Pashtuns and it was the images of those ethnic Afghans that really struck Clements . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I was looking at a photo essay of Canadian soldiers in Afghanistan . And I just started looking at the faces of the Indigenous people there and you know , in certain angles they looked like Indigenous people in Canada . This was really great . What would happen if these two ancient cultures that had survived so many wars were face to face ? And what does modern tribalism look like ? And how do we survive using our identity ? You know , as people ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The film was shot in 20 days ( some of those days the temperature dropped to -51 degrees ) on location outside of Yellowknife and here in B.C. near Ashcroft , Kamloops and Cache Creek . Four languages are spoken in the film : Pashtun , English , Gwich'in and Inuvialuktun. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;41591;TOOLONG ... title= " Galiano Island filmmaker Marie Clements ' latest film Red Snow was shot near Yellowknife ( seen here ) as well as in locations in the B.C. Interior . Photo credit : Samantha Hefford " alt= " Galiano Island filmmaker Marie Clements ' latest film Red Snow was shot near Yellowknife ( seen here ) as well as in locations in the B.C. Interior . Photo credit : Samantha Hefford " data-portal-copyright= " Samantha Hefford " **28;41627;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1223648 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Clements has been a writer in residence for many theatrical organizations and universities . Her work in the theatre has earned her awards and nominations and her plays have been translated into different languages . Right now she is busy developing screenplays , making music videos , working on a four-part miniseries with the CBC and a documentary with the NFB. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Everything&amp;rsquo ; s percolating . Everything&amp;rsquo ; s moving forward , &amp;rdquo ; said Clements . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Interactive digital media and community outreach components have been created as part of the Red Snow experience . The opening night gala on March 3 will feature the debut of two singles from the brand new Red Snow soundtrack . Dirt Walkers and We Are Still the People will be performed live by a band of Afghan and Canadian Indigenous musicians including : &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Mozhah Jamalzadah , Wayne Lavallee , Marina Hasselberg , Edris Fayaz and Majid Qiyam&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Telling Indigenous stories has always been the plan for Clements . Now she says the industry and the consumers have started to really catch up . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There&amp;rsquo ; s been a significant shift , &amp;rdquo ; said Clements . &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s really exciting to be a storyteller at this time . And I do think that those doors that were , you know , closed for so long are opening . And I think audiences , you know , want to engage in Indigenous stories . I think we&amp;rsquo ; re just looking to consume what we don&amp;rsquo ; t know . To see things that we haven&amp;rsquo ; t seen , you know , be involved in a dialogue that we might not have had even five years ago . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Canada , it&amp;rsquo ; s exploding with some really phenomenal directors and filmmakers and storytellers so I think it&amp;rsquo ; s a real sign that there&amp;rsquo ; s a critical mass of stories coming out that are high quality and really original and have a lot of craft , &amp;rdquo ; added Clements . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After the 7 p.m. screening of Red Snow Clements and some of her actors will be on hand for a Q&amp;amp ; A. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;41657;TOOLONG ... title= " Galiano Island filmmaker Marie Clement , seen here on set in the North West Territories , has plenty of irons in the fire including projects with the CBC and the NFB . Davis " **28;41693;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1224285 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Another Indigenous film worth highlighting at the festival is Unceded Chiefs . The documentary is making its Canadian premiere here at VIWFF on March 7 . The film from local director Doreen Manuel looks at the B.C. First Nations leaders who joined together to reject Prime Minister Pierre Elliot Trudeau&amp;rsquo ; s proposed 1969 White Paper Policy . That policy was setting out to abolish all legal documents that had previously existed , including the Indian Act , and all treaties within the country . Before designing and advocating mass-wood highrises , Green assisted C&amp;eacute ; sar Pelli on Kuala Lumpur&amp;rsquo ; s reinforced-concrete Petronas Towers . At 452 metres , the 1996 structures were the world&amp;rsquo ; s tallest until 2004 . Green recalls clients nixing Pelli&amp;rsquo ; s original design because his tower cross-sections resembled the six-pointed Star of David . When redrawn with two more to suggest the Muslim Rub El Hizb symbol , and with further facets added , Pelli got the go-ahead . Real EstateLocal ArtsLocal BusinessLocal HealthMedicine MattersMoviesReal EstateWomenBlockbuster film Sonic the Hedgehog provided $37.5M boost to B.C.http : **36;42537;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;42575;TOOLONG ... title= " Paramount Pictures ' Sonic the Hedgehog film injected $37.5 million into B.C. " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;42611;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1217255 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Many say you&amp;rsquo ; ve got to spend money to make money , and blockbuster film Sonic the Hedgehog did just that here in B.C. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Paramount Pictures film is racing its way to the top of the box office , but not before it spent $37.5 million in B.C. during its 2018 production . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; According to figures released Thursday by the Motion Picture Association of Canada , the production spent $22 million on local labour , creating 1,450 jobs for local cast and crew when it spent 53 days filming in B.C. two years ago ( figures in Canadian dollars unless otherwise noted ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The professional artists and technicians of IATSE Local 891 love a challenge and bringing this much loved video game character to the big screen fit the bill , &amp;rdquo ; said Phil Klapwyk with IATSE Local 891 , which represents film workers across B.C. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Sonic the Hedgehog isn&amp;rsquo ; t just a creative and technical success ; it provided good jobs for many B.C. crew members . We&amp;rsquo ; re definitely hoping for a sequel ! &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; An additional $15.5 million was injected into the local economy for production-related goods and services , including $490,000 on catering , $1.6 million on hotels and accommodations , $350,000 on hardware and lumber , and $300,000 on wardrobe . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Production for the film , which is based on the classic Sega video game franchise about a speedy blue hedgehog , took place across B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s South Coast , with the majority of its filming locations on Vancouver Island . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;42641;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;42677;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Campbell River&amp;rsquo ; s Shelter Point Distillery , Nanaimo&amp;rsquo ; s Diplomat Hotel , Belcarra&amp;rsquo ; s Camp Howdy and Squamish&amp;rsquo ; s Stawamus Chief Park all feature in the film . A key action sequence &amp;ndash ; which sees Jim Carrey&amp;rsquo ; s Dr. Ivo Robotnik chasing Sonic , voiced by Ben Schwartz , and his human companion played by James Marsden &amp;ndash ; was filmed in the area around Vancouver Island&amp;rsquo ; s Highway 19. &lt;a href= " https : **33;42713;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;The span was closed for five days in August 2018&lt;/a&gt; to allow for production to stage a number of vehicle-related stunts . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Filming in B.C. provided us with incredible local cast and crew , and beautiful locations for Sonic the Hedgehog , &amp;rdquo ; said Lee Rosenthal with Paramount Pictures . &amp;ldquo ; We found great partnership in the local business community , and they satisfied all of our production-related needs . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Sonic the Hedgehog hit theatres on Feb. 14 and raked in US $70 million over that country&amp;rsquo ; s four-day Presidents Day long weekend , &lt;a href= " https : **33;42748;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;according to the Hollywood Reporter&lt;/a&gt; . The film also debuted to US $43 million from its first 40 international markets . The film cost US $87 million to make . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The movie had initially been forecast to bring in US $45 to $50 million domestically over the four-day period , but beat that projection with US $58 million over just three days . Analysts now say the film has a chance at grossing US $200 million domestically . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A post-credits sequence also hinted at a possible continuation of the Sonic film franchise , after introducing Miles &amp;ldquo ; Tails&amp;rdquo ; Prower , a sidekick to Sonic and featured character in the original video games . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " mailto:sip@postmedia.com " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **31;42783;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **25;42816;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **31;42843;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;42876;TOOLONG ... title= " Actors James Marsden , Ben Schwartz , Jim Carrey and Tika Sumpter ( left to right ) at a Sonic The Hedgehog Family Day Event on Jan. 25 in Hollywood . " alt= " Actors James Marsden , Ben Schwartz , Jim Carrey and Tika Sumpter ( left to right ) at a Sonic The Hedgehog Family Day Event on Jan. 25 in Hollywood . " data-portal-copyright= " Courtesy , VIMFF " **28;43300;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1212802 " /&gt; &lt;h3&gt;Vancouver International Mountain Film Festival&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;When : &lt;/strong&gt; Feb. 21-March 1 . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Where : &lt;/strong&gt; Various locations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Tickets and info : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;a href= " https : //vimff.org/ " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;vimff.org&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; Rock climber and upcoming &lt;a href= " https : //vimff.org/ " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Vancouver International Mountain Film Festival ( VIMFF ) &lt;/a&gt; speaker &lt;a href= " https : **31;43330;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Shelma Jun&lt;/a&gt; sees the great outdoors as a great way to empower women . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Through her organization &lt;a href= " https : //flashfoxy.com/foxforce " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Flash Foxy&lt;/a&gt; and the &lt;a href= " https : //flashfoxy.com/wcf " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Women 's Climbing Festival ( WCF ) &lt;/a&gt; , the New Yorker is all about celebrating a growing community of &amp;ldquo ; badass women climbers . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A former urban planner and community organizer , Jun decided it would be great to get women together for an organized weekend of climbing , conversation and camaraderie . It turns out other women thought that was a great idea , too . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I thought there would be like 40 of us and it ended up being 200 , &amp;rdquo ; Jun said of the first event in Bishop , Calif. , in 2016. &amp;ldquo ; The Women&amp;rsquo ; s Climbing Festival has been amazing . It always sells out ( and ) there is several hundred women on the waiting list . The second year we had 800 on the wait list . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The WCF is held annually in two locations : Bishop and Chattanooga , Tenn. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Since beginning Flash Foxy in 2014 , Jun has gone on to be an outspoken and successful advocate for women climbers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One of the ways she does that is by working with the American Mountain Guide Association to help create opportunities for women to become guides . That initiative is just part of Jun&amp;rsquo ; s goal to get more women outside and more women listened to once they do get outdoors . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jun will be talking about the push for an equitable outdoors when she delivers her opening-night presentation at the VIMFF on Feb. 21 at 7:30 p.m. in North Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s Centennial Theatre . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I am really proud of creating a space that is challenging the status quo and creating opportunities for women , especially women from underrepresented groups , to have a voice in the outdoor space , &amp;rdquo ; Jun said recently from her home in Brooklyn . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She also makes movies , and those gathered in North Vancouver for her festival appearance will get a look at her latest work , Duality . The short film follows Jun on a climbing trip to her native South Korea . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;43363;TOOLONG ... title= " ' I am really proud of creating a space that is challenging the status quo and creating opportunities for women , especially women from underrepresented groups , to have a voice in the outdoor space , ' says Shelma Jun. " alt= " ' I am really proud of creating a space that is challenging the status quo and creating opportunities for women , especially women from underrepresented groups , to have a voice in the outdoor space , ' says Shelma Jun. " data-portal-copyright= " Sasha Turrentine " **28;43399;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1213417 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The idea was , if I go Korea and climb with Korean climbers , what will I feel ? Will it feel different ? Will I feel more connected ? Or will it not ? &amp;rdquo ; said Jun , who moved from South Korea to California at age five . &amp;ldquo ; That was kind of really the premise of this trip . Obviously there were some unexpected discoveries and situations , so this presentation I am doing on opening night is really about my story of being Korean American , of being a climber going on this trip and kind of what I started to discover from that and what I learned from it and how I am feeling moving forward . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The short film follows Jun &amp;mdash ; who counts as one of her main sponsors North Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s high-performance outdoor equipment company Arc&amp;rsquo ; Teryx &amp;mdash ; all over South Korea , including a climb near the border with North Korea . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think it is a very relatable story of using the landscape to connect and just having multiple identities , &amp;rdquo ; said Jun. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;43429;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;43465;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Jun grew up in California , and after completing her masters degree in urban planning at UCLA moved to New York City about eight and half years ago . Once in NYC she decided to take up rock climbing . And yes , she sees the irony of leaving California only to fall in love with rock climbing while living in Brooklyn . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She also discovered through climbing that she wanted and needed more meaningful relationships with women . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I found climbing here and an amazing group of women that I started climbing , learning and growing with , &amp;rdquo ; said Jun. &amp;ldquo ; I talked about it a little bit in other articles and things , but just kind of growing up a self-proclaimed tomboy I have always had these complicated relationships with women . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Those relationships began to change with each rock faced scaled . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think it is the kind of partnerships that climbing allows . I think it is being in my 30s and I think it is having the women I have found here . I think having female mentors , I think it was a perfect storm of a lot of things in my life that really allowed me to appreciate this and value this group of women that I found through rock climbing and that&amp;rsquo ; s what kind of started Flash Foxy . It started out not meaning to be anything specific , just my girlfriends and I climbing and celebrating that . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Then this thing called Instagram really took off and suddenly Flash Foxy was 40,000 followers strong . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think a lot of people that thought they were the only ones discovered all of these people through Instagram , &amp;rdquo ; said Jun. &amp;ldquo ; They found people who were like them and enjoyed things they enjoyed . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;43501;TOOLONG ... title= " Shelma Jun , shown climbing in South Korea last year . " alt= " Shelma Jun , shown climbing in South Korea last year . " data-portal-copyright= " Sasha Turrentine " **28;43537;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1213879 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jun feels climbing is the perfect activity to help encourage and empower women . Her festivals welcome women , ranging from those who don&amp;rsquo ; t know a carabiner from a nut to those who have a lifetime&amp;rsquo ; s worth of chalk under their fingernails . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s cool we can be all different levels and we can go out together and each of us can challenge ourselves and have a good time . That&amp;rsquo ; s one of the really special things about climbing , &amp;rdquo ; said Jun. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For her hard work , Jun in 2017 was named as one of Outside magazine&amp;rsquo ; s 40 women who have made the most impact on our world . In 2019 , Conde Nast Traveler put her on its 30 most powerful women in travel list . But those accolades , while nice , are not why she&amp;rsquo ; s in the game . She sees her life much like climbing , in that she makes one small move at a time , moves that will result in a bigger-picture win down the road . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I know I can&amp;rsquo ; t change the world at large but I am really lucky to be able to try to create the world I would like to see . I think that&amp;rsquo ; s a privilege not everyone has , &amp;rdquo ; said Jun , adding her focus is how she works hard to create workplaces where she would actually want to work . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m running this stuff . I&amp;rsquo ; m running these businesses . I&amp;rsquo ; m running these things . It is kind of a little bit of a challenge to make it in a way that feels good for me . You know , is it the kind of place where I would want to work even if I wasn&amp;rsquo ; t running it ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jun is just one of the nearly 30 accomplished storytellers and adventurers who will be taking part in the nine days of VIMFF . Founded in 1998 , the festival presents 70 films from B.C. and around the world . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The VIMFF also includes a rich program of clinics , workshops and fun outdoor activity days . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;43567;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **32;43594;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/danagee " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **29;43628;TOOLONG &; ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;43659;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;43701;TOOLONG ... talent Margaret Cho is unmasked for JFL **30;43736;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;43768;TOOLONG ... latest Metro Vancouver arts **25;43803;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;43830;TOOLONG ... in The Vancouver Sun&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;43866;TOOLONG ... for something to do ? They&amp;rsquo ; ve just been for TV , &amp;rdquo ; said Liebert referring to the films &lt;a href= " https : **31;44207;TOOLONG ? r ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Amish Abduction&lt;/a&gt; and a yet to be released Christmas feature she shot . &amp;ldquo ; So this is really special for me and , you know , my family&amp;rsquo ; s coming over from Vancouver Island and they&amp;rsquo ; ve never seen anything I&amp;rsquo ; ve done on a big screen either . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Liebert &amp;lsquo ; s 13 minute film The Quieting is one of six selected for this the 21st edition of the Crazy8s eight-day filmmaking event . The six films , chosen from 196 applicants , will be screened on Feb. 22 at The Centre in Vancouver . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The other five films are : Itsy Bitsy Spider from director Brodi-Jo Scalise ; Mr. James is Dead directed by Daniel Irving and Josh Aries ; Sol directed by Andy Alvarez ; The Substitute from Malibu Taetz and This is a Period Piece from Bruna Arbex. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Of the close to 200 teams that submitted a short video pitch 40 were then chosen to pitch in person to a jury of film industry professionals . After that 12 teams advanced to the script stage where they went through story editing sessions with professional story editors . From there the scripts were debated and championed by the final jury before the six finalists were announced at an event in early January . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There is really nothing like Crazy8s . Our entire local film and television industry rallies around the final six films and elevates the production possibilities , &amp;rdquo ; said Erin Mussolum co-executive director/executive producer of the Crazy8s Film Society . &amp;ldquo ; Crazy8s Film Society is a talent incubator . We want to give filmmakers as much experience as possible working with great gear houses , having them align with industry professionals , &amp;nbsp ; providing them union actors , and introducing them to the guilds and industry that are the backbone of our local industry . What can be done in just eight days is really astounding ! &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The result are films that lean heavily into the themes of &amp;ldquo ; climate change , self identity , psychological stresses and family dynamics , &amp;rdquo ; according to Mussolum. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;44240;TOOLONG ... title= " Writer/director Ali Liebert and Sara Canning at work on the new film The Quieting. " alt= " Writer/director Ali Liebert and Sara Canning at work on the new film The Quieting. " data-portal-copyright= " Courtesy of The Quieting " **28;44276;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1215686 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Liebert&amp;rsquo ; s The Quieting , which she also wrote , fits firmly into the self-identify column as it is based on her own story of coming out in her thirties . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In what Liebert calls a &amp;ldquo ; little time capsule of one night , &amp;rdquo ; the film has the 33-year-old newly queer Maggie ( Sara Canning ) go down a memory lane on the eve of an important date and confront her teenage self ( Julia Stone ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I haven&amp;rsquo ; t seen a lot of stories about people coming out sort of quote unquote later and actually it still feels like a turning point in my life , &amp;rdquo ; said Liebert , 39 , who came out five years ago . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She hopes that her efforts will resonate with others who are coming out or thinking about doing so . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I hope that it does inspire people to be who they are and come out at any age . It&amp;rsquo ; s not saying that you are not going to have to quiet those voices whether it is the fear of what other people are going to think or just your own internalized homophobia , or fear of judgment , but I think the movie ultimately , I hope , will be really uplifting , &amp;rdquo ; said Liebert , just hours after putting the final lock on the film . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Liebert , a Canadian Screen Award winning actor for her role in the Global series Bomb Girls , has plans on adding a regular directing career to her already busy acting life . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Yeah , eventually I&amp;rsquo ; d like to be a good enough director that I could direct or co direct myself in something , but I&amp;rsquo ; m not there yet . &amp;ldquo ; Yeah , I&amp;rsquo ; m just trying to figure out how to like , Bradley Cooper it , &amp;rdquo ; said Liebert with a laugh referring to the Star is Born star and director . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s worth noting that Liebert is in talks about a project that fits the above &amp;ldquo ; Bradley Cooper , &amp;rdquo ; bill . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the meantime Liebert is recovering from the crazy Crazy8s schedule and looking forward to a night with colleagues and family at the gala . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think even though the schedule is really crazy , I think it&amp;rsquo ; s pretty incredible that I will have like a completed short film in such a short amount of time . I think that this has helped me , not spend the next six months sort of like meandering , &amp;rdquo ; said Liebert , referring to being left to her own timeline. &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m a person that does better with deadlines , definitely . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **25;44306;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **32;44333;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/danagee " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **29;44367;TOOLONG &; ... &lt;/p&gt; Dana GeeMon , 17 Feb 2020 19:00:18 **45;44398;TOOLONG ... " data-portal-copyright= " Rich Polk " **28;45657;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1214076 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Filmmaker , author , actor , comic , podcaster and producer Kevin Smith is returning to his old alma mater. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It was while studying at the Vancouver Film School , Smith began work on his breakout indie hit Clerks with VFS alumni and creative collaborators Scott Mosier and Dave Klein . Clerks ( 1994 ) would launch the character of Silent Bob , Smith&amp;rsquo ; s half of the blunted duo of Jay ( played by Jason Mewes ) and Silent Bob . The two characters would subsequently appear in follow-up films such as Chasing Amy , Clerks II and Jay and Silent Bob Reboot. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Smith was quoted in a VFS press release , commenting on the crucial role the institution played in his progress : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Vancouver Film School was the first step on the road to making Clerks . In the hallowed halls of VFS , I not only learned the basics of my craft &amp;mdash ; I also met other likeminded dreamers who would go on to help me tell my stories for the rest of my life . I&amp;rsquo ; m proud and humbled to be named a Creative Ambassador for my alma mater , and I look forward to sharing what little I know with the next generation of VFS artists ! &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As a VFS creative ambassador , Smith will be making frequent appearances on campus to offer up mentorship and support to students , as well as a Storyteller&amp;rsquo ; s Studio AMA event . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The director , whose films have grossed millions at the global box office , is also behind a new scholarship initiative for aspiring creatives wanting to attend the Vancouver Film School . In 2019 , he awarded over $250,000 in writing , acting and film scholarships at the VFS. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; VFS president James Griffin announced Smith as the first VFS creative ambassador : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It is a remarkable event to have Kevin Smith as our first creative ambassador , &amp;rdquo ; said Griffin . &amp;ldquo ; Kevin attended VFS in the earliest days and has maintained a connection ever since . His success across film , TV , and digital media as writer , director , producer , actor , and celebrity is a testament to his immense talent . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 2018 , Smith played a role in saving the Rio Theatre from closure . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;45687;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;45723;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;45759;TOOLONG " **36;45790;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **27;45828;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **29;45857;TOOLONG &l; ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; Stuart DerdeynThu , 13 Feb 2020 19:22:18 **45;45888;TOOLONG ... things to see at the 2020 Talking Stick Festivalhttp : **32;45935;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;45969;TOOLONG ... title= " Raven Kanetakta and ShoShona Kish are Digging Roots , playing Feb. 22 , 8:30 p.m. at Roundhouse Exhibition Hall . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;46005;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1210545 " /&gt; &lt;h3&gt;Talking Stick Festival&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; **33;46035;TOOLONG : Feb. 18 &amp;ndash ; 29 &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Tickets and info&lt;/strong&gt; : Free to $30 at &lt;a href= " https : //talkingstickfest.ca/ " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **29;46070;TOOLONG &g; ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; Full Circle First Nations Performance has presented the Talking Stick Festival for the past 19 years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Founded by artist Margo Kane ( Cree/Saulteaux ) in 1992 , Full Circle&amp;rsquo ; s mission statement was to contribute to the development of Indigenous performance in Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Today , the festival extends that idea beyond national borders , presenting Indigenous artists from across the globe in performances that often focus on contemporary issues and experiences through a native lens . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This year&amp;rsquo ; s theme is ch&amp;eacute ; n ? chenstway , &amp;nbsp ; a Skwxwu7mesh Snichim/Squamish word meaning to uphold one another and to lift each other up . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; With a strong foundation in dance , the Talking Stick Festival also showcases music , theatre , film , visual arts and a lot of family activity . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Here are five things to see at this year&amp;rsquo ; s event : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;1 : RISING -- Ch ? n ? chenstway Visual Arts Exhibit&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **33;46101;TOOLONG : Feb. 18 &amp;ndash ; 29 , various times &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **34;46136;TOOLONG : Roundhouse Exhibition Hall &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Curated by Richard Heikkil&amp;auml ; -Sawan , this exhibit includes the work of visual artists working with textiles , sculpture , multimedia and painting . Year in and out , this exhibit tends to be one of the festival highlights and a great place to get introduced to rising stars of the local art scene . There is a free curator&amp;rsquo ; s talk on Feb. 22 at 1 p.m. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;2 : Wa ? hoks en Shqalawin ( Open Your Hearts ) Opening Gala&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **33;46172;TOOLONG : Feb. 20 , 7 p.m. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **34;46207;TOOLONG : Roundhouse Performance Centre &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **36;46243;TOOLONG : From $10 &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Can&amp;rsquo ; t really decide what your mood is ? NOTE : This almost always sells out quickly . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;3 : Isitwendam ( An Understanding ) &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **33;46281;TOOLONG : Feb. 26 , 27 , 28 , 1 and 7:30 p.m. ; Feb. 29 , 2 p.m. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **34;46316;TOOLONG : Roundhouse Performance Centre &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **36;46352;TOOLONG : From $10 &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Bound To Create Theatre and Native Earth Performing Arts present Meegwun Fairbrother&amp;rsquo ; s one-person play examining the legacy of damage from the Residential School system .
